The evidence index

Every row of docs/EVIDENCE.md, as it stands at 2f38cf32: what it shows, its numbers, where it ran, whether its proofs were real, how to re-check it and the file with the details.

How to read it (the index’s own introduction)

Read this first. One row per piece of evidence: what it shows, its numbers, where it ran, whether its proofs were real, how to re-check it, and the file with the details (every number below is taken from that file, or from the suite run of this commit for the last section). Words used for where it ran: in-process harness (LEZ v0.4.0-rc1's own state machine and a copy of its fee settlement, no sequencer), live rc1 sequencer (LEZ's unmodified v0.4.0-rc1 sequencer with a Logos Blockchain node), live rc2 sequencer (the same at v0.4.0-rc2), standalone rc3 sequencer (LEZ's v0.4.0-rc3 sequencer with its mock Bedrock, no Logos Blockchain node), local EVM (Foundry), fork of Sepolia (anvil), Sepolia (read-only calls, except the transactions of rows 26, 41, 91, 142, 154, 232, 244, 248 and 250), one L4 GPU (one NVIDIA L4 on Modal), build box (a Modal CPU container), laptop CPU, simulator, public LEZ testnet (LEZ v0.3.1 at testnet.lez.logos.co, row 150). Stand-in marks data we made ourselves where real data would go. Rows are numbered in the order they were added. Nothing here is audited. Since 21 November 2026: every proof in this index but those of rows 231 and 250 (RISC Zero 3.1, verified by RiscZeroGroth16Verifier 3.1.0, row 232; row 248's checkpoint is row 250's) is made with RISC Zero 3.0.5, whose recursion and Groth16 circuits accept forged claims (GHSA-g6qg-9j8p-7j87, GHSA-mm6c-35cm-g4x2, 11 November 2026): a recorded seal or receipt was made by our provers from its recorded inputs, but its acceptance by a 3.0.x verifier, on chain or off, shows only that it is well-formed (security note). Every transaction sent to a public network is on Sepolia, from the owner's runs (the exceptions: 11 transactions on the public LEZ testnet, row 150, and the LEZ transactions on our own public LEZ v0.4.0-rc3 test network, rows 244, 248 and 250 among them): the first attempt's one transaction, which ran out of gas, then the second run's deployment of the v3 vault and its deposit 0 (row 26), which a LEZ sequencer we run then claimed (row 27), and the round trip of the v5 vault, live on 11 November 2026: its deployment, two deposits, a checkpoint and two releases, 13 transactions (row 41); and the same round trip on the final programs, live on 12 November 2026, 13 transactions more (row 91); and with the LEZ side on LEZ v0.4.0-rc3, live on 18 November 2026, 13 more (row 142); and the owner's eight setGlobalCap transactions of 21 November 2026 (the deployer's nonces 49–56), which closed the four vaults (row 154); and on the test network's vault 0x19499061…2787, its deployment (row 232), a deposit through the served wallet (row 244) and the host's relayer's checkpoint, finalisation, release and fee claim (rows 248, 250). On a LEZ network Logos runs only the light client's experimental v0.3.1 port is deployed (row 150); none of the proposal's v0.4 programs is (LEZ testnet 0.4 does not exist): the round trip's LEZ side is a devnet we ran. The attestation program and the bridge program at B1 also run on a LEZ v0.4.0-rc3 network we run that is reachable publicly (rows 234, 237, 238); the first full round trip ran there too (row 250).

Row 43 monitor, the watchers' node-side program, guards a v5 vault on a local chain.

What it shows

Wrong candidates are cancelled before their deadline: a conflicting tip, a key set that is not the live one, a tip not in the final tier after the 300 s node-lag allowance. A monitor SIGKILLed mid-window and restarted from its state file still cancels; one that lost its state file recovers from the chain and makes exactly one cancellation. Under rule (A): a stale key set is cancelled at once, a tip not final by submittedAt + Δ_g is cancelled at Δ_g, and a stalled final tier brings the pause.

Design docs/design/messages.md: hybrid X25519 + ML-KEM-768 envelopes with XChaCha20-Poly1305, a fresh key per message, keys derived from the wallet key, padding buckets and spam limits enforced by the program; v2 stores one envelope per account so that strangers do not raise the owner's costs.
Numbers
(1) cargo test -p ferrata-lez-msg-v2-wire -p ferrata-lez-msg-wire --locked: 6 + 6 logic tests, the JS vectors decoded and re-encoded by each program's types, and the IDL vectors each writes; (2) node --test deploy/testnet/web/msg/test/*.test.mjs: 25 tests, among them an implementation sharing no code with the library (node:crypto, a hand-written HChaCha20) against OpenSSL 4.0.3's ML-KEM-768 values (test/kat.json), and the docs' own check (test/docs.test.mjs: every path they cite exists, every number here and in the design is the one its record prints); (3) RISC0_DEV_MODE=1 cargo test -p harness --features msg --locked --test messages_v2: 284 signed transactions through LEZ's state machine and fee settlement, 248 applied, 34 refused by the program with a code, 2 reverted by LEZ's validity window, no refusal changing any state, 21 of the program's 21 exit codes reached (BAD_STATE by a send addressed to one of the program's own inbox or envelope accounts), and every applied transaction within the clients' gas limits (client.js GAS_V2; an edit's limit grows with the ids it names), the worst cases it builds included: a send between full lists and a near-full inbox, list edits of 1 to 128 ids, with account ids and with ids sharing 31 bytes, a first publish on full lists, clears of 32 envelopes by an owner with full lists (lez/results/messages-v2.md); (4) ... --test messages_findings: the review's program findings measured the same way on both programs, each failing on v1 and passing on v2 (lez/results/messages-findings.md); (5) ... --test messages (v1): 19 of its 20 exit codes reached (BAD_STATE defensive; lez/results/messages.md); (6) deploy/testnet/web/msg/test/box.sh e2e ferrata_msg_v2.bin on LEZ v0.4.0-rc3's own standalone sequencer: 27 of 27 checks; deployed by LEZ's CLI (3 segments and a header); a send to an inbox account refused before sending; bulk block-list edits through the library (63 ids, then 128) applied within its limits; a forced stranger message and a forced non-contact message each reverted in its block (read from the block's reverted list; LEZ's RPC serves no exit codes, which the harness shows), nothing stored; contacts-only let a contact through; deletion emptied the envelope accounts; no message text in any state (deploy/testnet/web/msg/test/results/local-2/record.txt); (7) python3 -I idl/check.py: both IDLs decode every committed vector alone (idl/vectors/ferrata_msg.vectors.json, ferrata_msg_v2.vectors.json) and rederive v2's PDAs from their documented seeds. CI job msg runs (1), (2), (3)-(5) and the IDL generator's check. v1's own end-to-end record (test/results/local-1/record.txt, round 1) shows a forced stranger message not stored and its fee; it did not read exit codes and did not exercise contacts-only.
